Fixed structurally: `execute_pkg` now captures the entry-time Bybit leg id(s) via a before/after snapshot diff (`trades.sl_order_id`/`.tp_order_id`, NULL when ambiguous or pre-migration), `modify_open_order` amends that SPECIFIC leg in place via `amend_order` when a tracked id exists (no new leg created), and `close_open_position` best-effort cancels the closing trade's own tracked leg(s). A leg with no tracked id still falls back to the legacy qty-scoped add-a-leg `set_trading_stop` call (logged warning if a caller omits qty) — byte-for-byte the old behavior, now scoped to the untracked minority rather than every trade. The pre-existing stranded-leg backlog is a separate one-off cleanup (`cancel-stale-tpsl-legs` system-action, Tier-2, dry-run by default). **Tier-3 to flip `BYBIT_TPSL_MODE` itself on the live VM** (order-path semantics); gate: the `validate-partial-tpsl` operator action must PASS on the demo account (bybit_1) first. Unknown values resolve to `full`. **LIVE VALUE (verified 2026-07-30): `partial`** — confirmed three ways (`.env:81`, the unit's `EnvironmentFiles`, and authoritatively `/proc/<MainPID>/environ`), with `_bybit_tpsl_mode()` resolving `'partial'`. It has been live since roughly 2026-07-21: 75 of 211 pre-flip opens already carried a non-NULL `sl_order_id`, a column written ONLY on the partial branch. **So the 2026-07-30 05:37Z "flip" was a no-op re-assertion, NOT an activation** — do not attribute any behaviour change to it. `docs/research/exit-capture-deepdive-2026-07-30.md` assumes the opposite and is wrong; see `BL-20260730-EXITCAPTURE-DEEPDIVE-WRONG-TPSL-PREMISE`. |
| `NEWS_ENABLED` | **Removed 2026-06-10** — the legacy separate enable gate is gone (it was an "on by omission" footgun: code-default `true`). Activation is now **source-driven** (see `NEWS_SOURCE`); there is no on/off flag, and a leftover `NEWS_ENABLED` value in the environment is **ignored**. Per-symbol queries/keywords live in `config/news_symbols.yaml`; full reference in `docs/news_layer.md`. |
| `NEWS_SOURCE` | Feed backend **and the activation gate** for the **M9 news layer** (`src/news/`): `rss` (free, **keyless**, **real-time** — feeds in `config/news_feeds.yaml`; **always active when selected**) or `newsapi` (default; **active only when `NEWS_API_KEY` is set** — the free tier is ~24h delayed, so prefer `rss`). When the selected source is unusable (newsapi with no key), the layer is a cheap neutral no-op — it never blocks a trade. A live source **can** veto (`pipeline.py:477`), so selecting `rss` / setting a key is the deliberate activation. |
| `NEWS_API_KEY` | NewsAPI key — required only when `NEWS_SOURCE=newsapi`. Unused for `rss`. |
| `NEWS_VETO_ENABLED` | **Default `true` (on-by-omission) — a LIVE trade-blocking gate.** The news veto (`src/news/news_score.py::_get_veto_enabled`, checked in `pipeline.py` before `multi_account_execute`) blocks the signal for **every account incl. real money** when an item has `sentiment < NEWS_VETO_SENTIMENT_THRESHOLD` (−0.6) AND `impact > NEWS_VETO_IMPACT_THRESHOLD` (0.7). Inert when the news layer is inactive (default `NEWS_SOURCE=newsapi` + no key → no articles → no veto), but **armed whenever the source is active** (`NEWS_SOURCE=rss`) — so activating the source for soak data ALSO arms the veto (operator-confirmed intended, 2026-06-28: "selecting rss is the deliberate activation"). It is a per-trade refusal with a Telegram ping (Prime-Directive-correct shape), not an account-mode flip. Set `false`/`0`/`no` to disable. Tier-3 to change on the VM (a live trade-blocking condition). Distinct from the influence **sizing** half (`NEWS_INFLUENCE_MODE`, default `off` — observe-until-opt-in). |
| `NEWS_INFLUENCE_MODE` | Gate for the **graduated news-influence sizing** hook (`src/runtime/news_sizing.py`, applied in `Coordinator.multi_account_execute` after the advisory downsize). `off` (default) / `annotate` (no resize) / `downsize`. **Reductive-only** — shrinks the per-account qty toward `NEWS_INFLUENCE_SIZE_FLOOR` when the news (and any imminent event) opposes the trade direction, never enlarges. Inert when off or when the news layer isn't active. Design: `docs/news-influence-DESIGN.md`. Tier-3. |
| `NEWS_INFLUENCE_SIZE_FLOOR` / `NEWS_INFLUENCE_OPPOSE_THRESHOLD` / `NEWS_INFLUENCE_EVENT_RISK_WEIGHT` | Tuning knobs for the news-influence factor (defaults `0.5` / `0.05` / `0.5`): the smallest fraction a downsize may leave, the opposition dead-band, and how strongly an (injected) `event_risk` downsizes. Only consulted when `NEWS_INFLUENCE_MODE=downsize`. |
| `PROP_EXPIRY_PROMPT_SECONDS` / `PROP_EXPIRY_PROMPT_MAX_AGE_HOURS` | Knobs for the **prop ticket-expiry Yes/No prompt** (`src/prop/prop_expiry_prompt.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`). When a prop ticket passes its `valid_until` with no report-back, the bot asks the operator on the prop bot — inline **Yes/No** buttons (handled in `src/bot/claude_bridge.py` `propexp:*`): **No** → the ticket is logged `expired`; **Yes** → it moves to `awaiting_report` and the operator gets the `REPORT_PROMPT` to paste the fill (linked via `match_fill_to_ticket`, which now accepts `expiry_prompted`/`awaiting_report`). Lifecycle: `emitted → expiry_prompted → expired \| awaiting_report → filled/closed`. **Baseline, no enable gate** (Prime Directive); idempotent via the status flip (prompted exactly once, only after a confirmed send — no state file). `PROP_EXPIRY_PROMPT_SECONDS <= 0` pauses prompting; `PROP_EXPIRY_PROMPT_MAX_AGE_HOURS` (default `12`) bounds how stale a ticket may be before the bot stops asking, so a historical backlog can't spam on first deploy. Design: `docs/integrations/prop-telegram-inbound-DESIGN.md` § "Expired-ticket Yes/No prompt". |
| `PROP_INVALIDATION_PROMPT_SECONDS` / `PROP_INVALIDATION_PROMPT_MAX_AGE_HOURS` | Knobs for the **prop ticket price-invalidation prompt** (`src/prop/prop_invalidation_prompt.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`; operator-requested 2026-07-16). While the bot is still awaiting the operator's place-decision Yes/No on a freshly-**emitted** prop ticket, price can move **beyond the ticket's `[SL, TP]` brackets** — the described entry is no longer a live setup (a run to SL means the trade already failed; a run to TP means the move already happened). This fires a **proactive** prop-bot warning — `🚫 PROP SETUP NO LONGER VALID … ⚠️ Do NOT place this trade if you haven't already. Did you already place it?` — with the SAME Yes/No keyboard as the expiry prompt (shares the `propexp:*` callback → `handle_expiry_callback`): **No** → `expired`; **Yes** → `awaiting_report` → the fill-paste prompt. Distinct trigger from the `PROP_EXPIRY_PROMPT_*` **timeout** path — this catches an invalidated setup *before* `valid_until` elapses. Lifecycle: `emitted ──(price beyond brackets)──▶ invalidated_prompted ──┬─No→expired └─Yes→awaiting_report`. Idempotent: it scans `emitted` tickets only, so a flip to `invalidated_prompted` drops it out of BOTH this path and the timeout path's `find_unacted_tickets` (no double-prompt); the flip happens only after a confirmed send. Reuses the `prop_sl_tp_alert` price-fetch + crossing logic. **Baseline, no enable gate** (Prime Directive); `PROP_INVALIDATION_PROMPT_SECONDS <= 0` pauses it, `PROP_INVALIDATION_PROMPT_MAX_AGE_HOURS` (default `12`) bounds how old an emitted ticket may be before the timeout path (not this one) owns it — so a backlog can't spam on first deploy. Design: `docs/integrations/prop-telegram-inbound-DESIGN.md` § "Price-invalidation prompt". |
| `PROP_STATUS_REQUEST_MAX_AGE_HOURS` / `PROP_STATUS_REQUEST_COOLDOWN_HOURS` | Freshness threshold (default `24`h; `<= 0` pauses) + re-ask cooldown (default `12`h) for the prop **account-status request** — the manual bridge has no broker balance feed, so when the latest `prop_account_status` snapshot is missing or older than the threshold, the bot asks the operator for a fresh `bal <balance> <equity>` (`src/prop/prop_status_request.py`, once per trader tick). ⚠️ **AN OPEN POSITION IS CONTEXT, NOT THE TRIGGER** (corrected 2026-08-14, Tier-2 — this row previously read "while a prop position is open", which described a real defect the code has since been fixed to stop having). `run_prop_status_request` used to bail on `if not positions: return []` **and prune its cadence state to accounts holding an open position**, so the moment the prop book went flat the bot stopped asking and the snapshot aged without bound. The two prop limits are not both position-scoped: the daily-loss limit is a per-day account drawdown, but the **static DD floor** (`config/prop_rulesets/breakout.yaml`: `drawdown_type: static`, `max_drawdown_pct: 0.06` on `account_size_usd: 5000` ⇒ a **$4,700 floor**) is an **account-level** line that binds while FLAT — and flat is exactly when the next ticket is about to be sized against a cushion nobody has measured. The ask is now driven by `prop_identity.declared_prop_account_ids(live_only=True)` unioned with any account actually holding an open prop position (so an id config no longer declares is still covered), and the cooldown is what bounds the cost: one ping per account per 12h, only while the snapshot is genuinely stale. A position-scan **failure** rides down as `None`, never `[]` — the ping renders "could not read open positions" distinctly from "no open positions", and `emit_prop_status_request`'s payload carries `positions_known` so a consumer cannot read an unread book as an empty one. The SAME threshold also gates the **folded balance nudge** appended to every fill ack (`telegram_report_handler.account_status_nudge`) **and** `prop_balance.max_age_hours()` (the sizing-freshness gate) **and** the `status_freshness` verdict on `/api/bot/prop/status` — four consumers, one definition of "too old to trust", so sizing can never run off a balance the safety panel has written off. Baseline, no enable gate. ⚠️ **AND THE TRIGGER IS NOW ACTIVITY, NOT THE WALL CLOCK — the paragraph above describes WHICH ACCOUNTS are covered and is unchanged; WHEN the ask fires changed on 2026-09-09 (MI-214, operator-directed, PR #11530).** A stale snapshot is necessary but **no longer sufficient**: `prop_status_request.assess_activity` (a pure function, so the policy is arguable in tests rather than against a live prop account) grades five never-collapsed states and only **`quiescent`** suppresses the ask — `position_open` (an open position, whose UNREALIZED drawdown is invisible to the cushion and is the one genuinely unsafe case), `ticket_since_snapshot` (a real, non-`suppressed` ticket created after the snapshot — new exposure taken against this cushion, which is the 2026-08-14 static-DD purpose above OBSERVED rather than assumed), `no_snapshot`, and **`unknown` — *we could not look*, which ASKS**, because a read failure must never silence a safety prompt (`prop_monitor_pulse.find_open_prop_positions` swallows its own `list_fills` failure into `[]`, so an empty read is not evidence of quiescence). ⚠️ **A REPORTED FILL IS DELIBERATELY NOT A TRIGGER, and that is the load-bearing claim**: `prop_reconcile.reconstruct_equity` is ASYMMETRIC by design — an unplaceable LOSS is always applied, an unplaceable GAIN is withheld and counted — so a reported fill can only ever make the cushion **conservative**, never optimistic. ⚠️ **THE THRESHOLD DID NOT CHANGE**: the four consumers above still share one definition of "too old to trust"; only the trigger moved. Operator, 2026-09-09: *"This ping in the prop channel is unessacry, the account snapshot is updated when a trade closes, if there are no prop trades than the account state didn't change"* — ⚠️ and that premise is **half false**, recorded rather than inherited: MEASURED 2026-09-09T09:54Z on `/api/bot/prop/fills?limit=500`, `breakout_1`'s newest fill (id 41, `2026-08-30T19:39:00Z`) is **5.5 minutes NEWER** than the newest snapshot (id 19, `2026-08-30T19:33:29Z`), so a close *prompts* a snapshot and never guarantees one. ⚠️ **GOING QUIET WHILE FLAT IS ONLY SAFE BECAUSE SIZING HARD-REFUSES ON A STALE BALANCE, AND THAT REFUSAL IS JOURNALLED BUT PRACTICALLY UNALERTABLE** — `coordinator.py`'s sizing branch calls `log_rejection_to_journal` + `logger`, and the operator-facing `_emit_execution_failure_ping` is on the LATER `execute_pkg` branch, never reached (`BL-20260909-PROP-SIZING-REFUSAL-ON-A-STALE-BALANCE-IS-JOURNALED-BUT-NEVER-PINGED`). ⚠️ **THIS ROW READ 'AND THAT REFUSAL REACHES NOBODY' UNTIL 2026-09-09T23:3xZ AND THAT WAS FALSE — do not re-quote it.** The row it cites was CORRECTED IN PLACE by its own author the same day: `log_rejection_to_journal` → `_log_trade_to_journal` writes to `trades`, which is exactly what `silent_refusal_alert._read_window` reads, so the refusal DOES reach an operator-facing detector. Established by RUNNING the real code — `classify_cause` on the genuine string from `prop_balance.refusal_message` returns a real bucket and `assess()` grades the account `signalled_never_placed` — not by reading it. ⚠️ **WHAT SURVIVES IS NARROWER AND IS STILL A GAP, so this is a correction and not an all-clear:** the dedicated PING path is genuinely not reached, and the detector that does see it needs `verdict == signalled_never_placed` AND `SILENT_REFUSAL_MIN_ROWS` (5 in 24h) with **no `CAUSE_MIN_ROWS` override for `prop_balance_unreported`** — deliberately, because that event has occurred ZERO times ever and there are no run lengths to size a lowered floor from (the `balance_unreadable` override was justified by 11 measured occurrences). So one refusal grades `below_min_rows` and does not alert. ⚠️ **AND NONE OF THIS HAS BEEN OBSERVED:** measured 2026-09-09, `breakout_1` has ZERO rows in `trades` in the 1000-row window and ZERO rows anywhere contain `prop_balance` — the prop stale-balance refusal has never fired at all, because tickets are suppressed at `breakout_executor`'s reticket check, which runs BEFORE sizing. Whether that floor should be lowered is an OPEN OPERATOR DECISION recorded in the backlog row, not a defect to fix on precedent. |
| `PROP_FILLS_STALENESS_CHECK_SECONDS` / `PROP_FILLS_STALENESS_BALANCE_DELTA_USD` / `PROP_FILLS_STALENESS_CROSSED_GRACE_HOURS` / `PROP_FILLS_STALENESS_SKIP` | Knobs for the **prop FILLS-staleness alert** (`src/prop/prop_fills_staleness.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`; P1 of the 2026-08-23 prop refinement). `PROP_STATUS_REQUEST_*` above chases a stale **balance**; **nothing chased stale FILLS** — which is why three days of terminal prop trades went unrecorded when the report-back path itself broke (the screenshot reader was 404-ing on a wrong model id) and were found only because the operator happened to send another screenshot (`BL-20260823-PROP-JOURNAL-MISSING-THREE-DAYS-OF-TERMINAL-TRADES`). The journal said the book was quiet; the venue disagreed by **$111.86**. ⚠️ **IT MUST NEVER KEY ON UNACTED TICKETS, AND DOES NOT** (operator-directed twice, 2026-08-23: an unanswered ticket is the **expected** shape on a manual bridge, and ticket answer-rate *"shouldn't be a metric of success here"*). An alert keyed on them would fire constantly on correct behaviour — the desensitized-alarm P1. Both detectors therefore require **positive evidence the book traded**, and a test asserts the module never imports `list_tickets` / `find_unacted_tickets` / `prop_reconcile`. **TWO DETECTORS, deliberately not one:** **A `crossed_unreported`** — an open position whose SL/TP was *already announced as crossed* by `prop_sl_tp_alert` at least `..._CROSSED_GRACE_HOURS` (default `6.0`) ago and which is *still* open in the journal; the crossing is a price observation, not an assumption, so this fires DURING the blind window. **B `balance_moved_unreported`** — two consecutive operator-reported `prop_account_status` snapshots whose `balance` differs by at least `..._BALANCE_DELTA_USD` (default `25.0`) with **zero** fills reported in the interval; a moved balance is realized PnL, so closes happened. B caught the actual incident and **cannot punish unavailability** — it only fires once the operator HAS reported. ⚠️ **B does NOT require the fills to RECONCILE with the delta**: measured on the live table, the 2026-08-19 pair moved **+$245.00** against **+$235.97** of reported fills and the 07-20→08-18 pair **−$78.61** against **−$68.61** — both correct-and-explained, and a reconciliation test would have called both a finding. The question is *was anything reported*, not *does it add up*. **THRESHOLD BASIS (state the population):** over the **10 consecutive snapshot pairs** in the live table on 2026-08-23 the deltas were −27.27, −72.00, −186.72, −1.28, −102.39, −78.61, −9.00, +245.00, −0.14, **−111.86**; seven had fills reported and are explained, three did not (−9.00, −0.14, and the real incident). **$25 fires exactly once on this history, on the true positive** — above the observed unexplained noise floor and far below the $150 daily-loss limit. $5 adds one alert, $0.01 adds two. A **chosen** value with a measured basis, not a tuned one; n=10 is a small denominator. **States are not collapsed:** `balance_state` ∈ `insufficient_snapshots` (fewer than 2 rows — no delta EXISTS, **not** "clean") · `balance_unreadable` (a non-numeric balance, **or an undateable snapshot** — without a window we cannot say what was reported inside it) · `within_noise` · `explained` · `unreported`; `crossing_state` ∈ `unknown` (**`prop_sl_tp_alert` has no entry for this position — we did not look**, never "did not cross") · `not_crossed` · `crossed_within_grace` · `crossed_unreported`. A journal-read failure grades nothing and latches nothing (`checked:false`, `reason:"read_failed"`/`"tables_absent"`) — never "no account is missing fills". Latched per **(account, finding-key)** like `silent_refusal_alert`, so a new finding on an already-latched account is not swallowed; a `crossed_unreported` latch clears with an `[OK]` when the position leaves the open set (the close WAS reported), while a `balance_moved_unreported` latch is pruned **silently** — a newer snapshot supersedes the evidence without repairing the journal, so an `[OK]` there would be a false statement. Cadence/threshold knobs, **not** a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive), and an unparseable value **falls back to its default rather than to zero**. `SKIP` is a CSV escape hatch mirroring `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP`. Read-only (one SQLite read per cadence window, no socket, no order path); state latch `runtime_logs/prop_fills_staleness_state.json`. Surfaced as the **`prop_fills_stale`** banner on `/api/bot/notifications` and via `stale_fill_accounts()` for the review skills. |
| `PROP_SCREENSHOT_MODEL` | Claude vision model used to read a **terminal screenshot** the operator sends to the prop bot into a structured report-back (`src/prop/screenshot_parse.py`, wired via `src/bot/claude_bridge.py::_on_operator_photo` → `telegram_report_handler.handle_screenshot`; operator-requested 2026-07-11). Default `claude-sonnet-5` (accuracy on money numbers). The extractor is **honest-null** (omits any field not visible — "Used Margin"/"Open P/L" are NOT the account balance/realized pnl), routes through the one `prop_report.ingest_report` chokepoint, and a single portfolio screen can yield a fill **and** an account_status. Best-effort: no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / bad image / unparseable output → a readable "type it instead" reply, never a crash (`anthropic` is already a dep from M13). Design: `docs/integrations/prop-telegram-inbound-DESIGN.md` § "Screenshot report-back". |
| `PROP_MONITOR_PULSE_SECONDS` | Cadence (seconds, default `3600` = hourly; was `900`/15-min, streamlined 2026-07-08) of the **prop monitoring pulse** — a periodic **consolidated** "still monitoring" heartbeat covering ALL open prop trades in ONE ping (`src/prop/prop_monitor_pulse.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`). Because the prop account is a manual bridge with no broker feed, the per-tick `order_monitor` never sees prop positions; this pulse reassures the operator the system is still actively tracking the trades between report-backs ("still monitoring N open prop trades · no change" + a per-position line), without replacing the real-time `prop_fill`/`prop_closed` events (event kind `prop_monitor`, Telegram via the prop bot + FCM). Operator directive 2026-07-08 turned it from per-position/15-min to **once an hour, one ping with all the open trades** (`emit_prop_monitor_consolidated`). **Baseline (no enable gate)** — the only knob is this cadence; set `<= 0` to pause pulses without a redeploy. Cadence state is a single-key `runtime_logs/prop_monitor_pulse.json` (`{"__consolidated__": last_iso}`; reset to empty when nothing is open). |
| `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS` / `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_THRESHOLD` / `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP` | Knobs for the **broker-account-down latched alert** (`src/runtime/account_reachability_alert.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`; BL-20260629-ACCOUNT-DOWN-ALERT). A supposed-to-be-live broker account reading **unreachable** (IB gateway logged out, exchange API 401-ing, creds rotated out) now fires its OWN loud, latched operator alert — one `🔴 [ALERT] DOWN` on a confirmed cross-into-down, one `🟢 [OK]` on recovery — instead of going unflagged (the IB gateway was dark across reviews and surfaced only quietly). Reachability uses the SAME primitive the reverse reconciler calls each tick (`account_open_positions`: `None` ⇒ down, list ⇒ up). Scope is **all declared-live, non-shelved accounts** — `mode: live` on a probeable exchange (`bybit`/`interactive_brokers`/`alpaca`/`oanda`), which excludes the dry/shelved `ib_live`/`oanda_practice` and the API-less `breakout_1`. `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS` (default `600` = 10 min) is the probe cadence — **on by default** (it's observability, not a trade gate; the same shape as `PROP_MONITOR_PULSE_SECONDS`), `<= 0` pauses it without a redeploy. `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_THRESHOLD` (default `2`) is how many consecutive down reads confirm an outage before the DOWN ping fires (so a transient blip never pings). `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP` is a CSV of account-ids to skip (escape hatch for a live account intentionally expected-down). The alert fires one Telegram + one typed `WARNING` FCM push (the loud Warning channel). Latch state is `runtime_logs/account_reachability_alert_state.json` (persists across restarts so the consecutive-down counter survives a bounce); `account_reachability_alert.down_accounts()` exposes the currently-latched-down set for the health-review / system-review skills, which now treat any down live account as a **mandatory** standalone flag. |
| `SILENT_REFUSAL_CHECK_SECONDS` / `SILENT_REFUSAL_WINDOW_HOURS` / `SILENT_REFUSAL_MIN_ROWS` / `SILENT_REFUSAL_SKIP` | Knobs for the **silent-refusal latched alert** (`src/runtime/silent_refusal_alert.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`; Tier-2, 2026-08-14). Closes the gap **between** the two checks above it: `/health-review`'s strategy-silence check measures `*_eval` events (a leg that evaluates, signals, and has every order refused is **not silent** — it is loudly failing at the last step, and grades `ok`), while `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS`'s probe reads `positions()` (an account whose `positions()` answers while `balance()` returns None reads **UP** while refusing every signal routed to it — `BL-20260814-REACHABILITY-PROBES-POSITIONS-NOT-BALANCE`). So "declared live, evaluates, signals, places NOTHING" was observable by neither. **Not hypothetical:** measured on the live journal 2026-08-14, `alpaca_live` produced **120 refusals across 16 separate days** at 3–5/hr and nothing alerted once; `BL-20260813-ALPACA-BALANCE-NONE-WHILE-ACCOUNT-READS-ACTIVE` records its own class was "found incidentally while verifying an unrelated fix". ⚠️ **THIS DOES NOT PROBE THE BROKER** — `account_reachability_alert`'s documented "No new exchange round-trip pattern" invariant is deliberately preserved, because the obvious fix (have the probe also call `balance()`) adds a per-account broker round-trip to a live tick, which is the shape of both June 2026 wedges. It reads the journal the trader has **already written**: one read-only SQLite connection on its own cadence, no socket. **Per ACCOUNT, not per leg** — `alpaca_live` routes 16 live strategies and a per-leg alert would fire 16 pings for one cause, which is the desensitized-alarm P1; one ping names the account, the dominant cause, and the legs. Verdicts come from `src/runtime/dead_leg.py`, the **same** module `scripts/ops/dead_leg_audit.py` grades with, so the live alert and the offline report can never disagree about a row — and the **four** states stay apart: **no rows at all** (we observed nothing — never graded, never alerts) vs **rows, all refused** (`signalled_never_placed`, the finding) vs **rows, some placed** (a refusal *rate*, a tuning question) vs **rows, all refused BY DECLARATION** (`refusing_by_declaration` — the account is `mode: dry_run`, so refusing every order IS the execution gate working). ⚠️ **THAT FOURTH STATE WAS MISSING FOR TEN DAYS AND COST A THREE-DAY FALSE ALARM** (2026-08-24, `BL-20260824-SILENT-REFUSAL-CANNOT-SEE-A-DECLARED-DRY-RUN`): this detector shipped 2026-08-14 claiming to watch for *"declared live, evaluates, signals, places NOTHING"* while **never establishing "declared live"** — so it latched a `real_money`-labelled alarm on `alpaca_live` from 2026-08-21T12:38Z for behaviour that was correct, the desensitized-alarm P1 in its purest form. The repo had **already ruled on this exact account**: `execution_diagnostics.EXPECTED_DISPATCH_SKIP_REASONS` was added by operator directive 2026-07-15 for the same reason, and 162 of 186 `alpaca_live` rows carry the `dry_run_sizing_skip` token it recognises. `dead_leg.bucket_for` now takes the row's **reason** and buckets a declared policy skip as `policy_skipped`, **importing** that predicate rather than re-deriving it — a second copy of "what counts as a declared skip" is exactly how the two would drift. Fail-**SAFE**, the opposite polarity to `account_side_filter`: that gates an *order* and is permissive, this gates an *alarm*, so an unrecognised reason stays a real refusal. Suppression is per-**ROW**, so a switched-off account still alerts on a genuine refusal (a venue cap, a zero balance) mixed in among its policy skips. Verified against the live journal over the 28 daily windows in which `alpaca_live` produced rows: 24 of 28 — every window since the prefix shipped, including the one that latched — now grade `refusing_by_declaration`, and the 2 pre-prefix windows still alert, correctly. **Read `alert_disposition`, never a bare `alerting: False`** — that boolean collapsed three different facts (`alerting` · `suppressed_declared_dry_run` · `below_min_rows` · `not_a_finding`), so "we are not alerting" could not be told from "we found nothing". Latched per **`(account, cause)`**, not per account: an account that stops refusing for `zero_balance` and starts refusing for a venue cap has a NEW problem, and a per-account latch would report it as "already alerting" and say nothing. `CHECK_SECONDS` (default `3600`) is the cadence, `WINDOW_HOURS` (default `24`) the lookback, `MIN_ROWS` (default `5`) what separates a pattern from one bad order (below it the account is still assessed and readable via `silent_accounts()`, just silent) — ⚠️ **but it is no longer the only floor** (2026-08-25, operator decision on `BL-20260825-BALANCE-UNREADABLE-CAN-NEVER-REACH-ITS-OWN-ALERT-THRESHOLD`): `silent_refusal_alert.CAUSE_MIN_ROWS` carries PER-CAUSE overrides, today `balance_unreadable: 1`, because a global floor is right for a volume pattern and categorically wrong for a cause that arrives in ones and twos weeks apart — there it does not mean *wait for a pattern*, it means *never fire*. Measured over the full journal that cause is **48 rows / 11 occurrences / max run 20**, and **0 of the 7 occurrences since 2026-07-01** (all runs of 1–3) would have alerted at 5. The map is consulted as an **ADDITIONAL** trip path, so it can only ADD alerting, never suppress — a value above the global floor silences nothing, deliberately, so this can never become a quiet way to disarm a cause. Read `alerting_basis` ∈ `total_floor|per_cause_floor|both|None` to see WHICH floor let a given alert through, and `priority_causes` for the rare cause that tripped its own — it joins the latch key, because the DOMINANT cause can bury the serious one (measured: `mgc_trend_1h` carries 3 `balance_unreadable` behind 7 `risk_refused`). ⚠️ **The floor is only HALF the gate and coverage is PARTIAL** — an alert also requires `verdict == signalled_never_placed`, so an account that placed even one order in the window never alerts however rare its cause; measured on the 2026-08-13 event, that leaves `alpaca_paper` uncovered while `ib_paper` and `alpaca_portfolio` are covered (`BL-20260825-RARE-CAUSE-INVISIBLE-ON-A-PARTIALLY-REFUSED-ACCOUNT`). `SKIP` a CSV escape hatch mirroring `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP`. `SKIP` a CSV escape hatch mirroring `ACCOUNT_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP`. Cadence/threshold knobs, **not** a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive), and an unparseable value **falls back to the default rather than pausing** — a typo must not silently switch off the only thing watching this class. A journal-read **failure** latches nothing and recovers nothing (`checked:false, reason:"read_failed"`), never "no account is refusing". State latch is `runtime_logs/silent_refusal_alert_state.json` (survives restarts); `silent_accounts()` exposes the latched set for the review skills, mirroring `down_accounts()`. |
| `TRAINER_HEARTBEAT_CHECK_SECONDS` / `TRAINER_DOWN_STALE_SECONDS` / `TRAINER_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP` | Knobs for the **trainer-VM-down latched alert** (`src/runtime/trainer_reachability_alert.py`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`; operator-requested 2026-07-08 after the trainer went SSH-dead across reviews unflagged). The trainer rsyncs `trainer_status.json` into the mirror every ~2 min (`ict-trainer-publish.timer`), so a mirror stale beyond `TRAINER_DOWN_STALE_SECONDS` (default `1200` = 20 min ≈ 10 missed publishes; clamped `>= 300`) is a **confirmed DOWN** — the staleness window is itself the confidence, no consecutive-reads counter needed. Latched: one `🔴 [ALERT] Trainer VM DOWN` (Telegram + one loud `WARNING` FCM push) on the cross-into-stale, one `🟢 [OK]` on recovery; also surfaced on `/api/bot/notifications` (the app banners) + `trainer_reachability_alert.status()`/`is_down()` for the review skills. **On by default** (observability, not a trade gate — same shape as `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS`/`PROP_MONITOR_PULSE_SECONDS`); `TRAINER_HEARTBEAT_CHECK_SECONDS` (default `300` = 5 min) is the check cadence, `<= 0` pauses it. `TRAINER_DOWN_ALERT_SKIP` truthy is the escape hatch for an intentional trainer-down window (re-provision/migration). State latch is `runtime_logs/trainer_reachability_alert_state.json` (survives restarts). Reads a local mirror file — no trader→trainer SSH, no scheduled workflow. |
| `TICK_COST_WRITE_SECONDS` | Persist cadence (seconds, default `300`; `<= 0` keeps measuring in memory but stops writing the file) for the **per-tick cost measurement** (`src/runtime/tick_cost.py`, `begin_tick`/`end_tick` bracketing the hook chain in `src/main.py`). The ACCUMULATOR runs every tick regardless of this cadence — only the write is gated — so the recorded `max_ms` never loses a spike that happened between two persists (the same reasoning as `EXPOSURE_SOAK_SECONDS`, where the max is load-bearing and must survive the sampling gap). Unparseable → falls back to the default rather than disabling, so a typo cannot silently switch the measurement off. Cost is two `time.monotonic()` calls per tick plus one atomic write of a FIXED-SIZE payload per cadence window (the accumulators do not grow with uptime). **Measurement only — enforces no budget.** Surfaced at `/api/diag/tick_cost`. |
| `CANDLE_CACHE_TTL_FRACTION` / `CANDLE_CACHE_TTL_MAX_S` | Staleness bound for the **per-process market-data caches** (`src/runtime/market_data.py`, 2026-08-10, BL-20260810-TICK-CHAIN-260S-PER-TICK). Default `0.10` / `60.0`; a cached OHLCV frame may be served for `min(bar_seconds * fraction, cap)` — the tolerable age scales with the bar, because a frame's only staleness risk is its last still-forming bar. `0` on the **fraction** serves every request fresh (the rollback path: one env flip + restart, no redeploy); `0` or an unparseable value on the **cap** falls back to `60.0` rather than disabling, so the two vars never share an off-semantics (the documented off-switch is the fraction, and a mistyped cap must not silently stop caching). **LIVE VALUE (Tier-3, operator-approved 2026-08-13): `CANDLE_CACHE_TTL_MAX_S=300`** — raised from the code default `60.0` via `set-env` #8949, trader restarted `active`. **The justification is the DECLARED FRACTION, not a tick comparison.** An earlier draft of this row argued 300s was "the same order as the tick's own staleness", calibrated against a 187.2s max tick; the M20 decouple cut the tick to 69.3s mean / 96.8s max, so that argument no longer holds and must not be re-quoted. What holds: the fraction declares tolerable staleness as 10% of the bar, and at cap 300 every affected frame is INSIDE its own tolerance — 1h **8.33%** (the binding frame), 2h 4.17%, 4h 2.08%, 1d 0.35%. 15m is unaffected (min(90,300)=90s, still under the 126.1s cycle, so it correctly keeps missing) and 5m/1m are untouched. ✅ **VERIFIED EFFECTIVE 2026-08-13** (warm read, `ticks_measured: 4`, process 08:04:17Z, diag #8954 vs the n=19 pre-flip baseline): cache hit rate **21.5% → 41.5%**, capped frames (1h+2h+4h+1d) **−39.5% per tick** against an unaffected 15m/5m/1m control at **−14.5%** — the differential is what carries it, not the raw drop. Per frame: 1h −40.6%, 2h −53.7%, 4h −52.5%. Tick mean 69.3s → 64.1s. ⚠️ **TWO CAVEATS, both load-bearing:** `ticks_measured: 4` is a small sample and these are shares, not a distribution; and **`fetch.1d` did NOT drop (+6.5%)** — the longest bar, where the cap should help most. ✅ **NOW EXPLAINED, and 1d is behaving CORRECTLY — `BL-20260813-1D-FRAME-DID-NOT-CACHE-AFTER-CAP-RAISE` is RESOLVED** (n=40 on one process, `process_started_utc` 2026-08-14T05:17:25Z, diag #9170). At that sample the capped siblings fell **1h −57.9% / 2h −64.7% / 4h −65.3%** against their pre-flip rates while **1d moved −0.8%** (3.02 vs 3.05 per tick) — real, not the small-sample noise the n=4 read left open. **The cause is arithmetic, not a cache defect: there are SIXTEEN enabled 1d strategies, each pinned to its own symbol, so 121 fetches / 16 symbols = 7.6 per symbol over 40 ticks, i.e. a per-symbol revisit interval of ~692s against a 300s TTL.** Every 1d entry expires on its own TTL before that symbol is asked for again, so the cache cannot hit and the 60→300 raise could not have helped — 300 is still under half the revisit interval. 1h/2h/4h improved precisely because their revisit intervals fall inside 300s. **Note the denominator that matters is the tick INTERVAL (131s = 87.2 min / 40 ticks), not the 72.3s mean tick DURATION** — the TTL competes with the interval, and conflating the two is how 1d looked like a defect. Two candidate causes were REFUTED, not merely unselected: **no caller passes `since=`** on the signal path (0 hits over `src/`, and `limit` is 200 at 22 of 26 sites, so key fragmentation is immaterial), and **off-loop dominance is not the cause** (off-loop `fetch.1d` is exactly 2.00× on-loop, but the on-loop rate is fully accounted for by the revisit interval alone). **If 1d caching is ever wanted, the cap must exceed ~700s** — which is well inside 1d's own declared tolerance (10% of an 86400s bar = 8640s; even 900s is 1.0%) — but the VALUE stays **Tier-3** for the reasons below, so that is a proposal, not a default. Upside is bounded at 15.5% of on-loop tick time. ⚠️ Separately noted while reading: `_candle_cache_put` bounds the map by **clearing it entirely** at >512 entries rather than evicting LRU; not what affects 1d here, but a wholesale flush disproportionately harms exactly the long-revisit frames, so it matters if the cap is raised. Do not read the headline hit-rate as covering 1d. The earlier `get-env` route (the key was absent from `get_env.py::ALLOWED_KEYS` until 2026-08-13 — its first live write could not be read back, `BL-20260813-ENV-VARS-SHIP-WITHOUT-A-READ-SURFACE`). Rollback is one env flip: `CANDLE_CACHE_TTL_FRACTION=0`, or this key back to `60`. **THE CAP IS THE BINDING TERM, NOT THE FRACTION** (measured 2026-08-12, 50 ticks, one process, `/api/diag/tick_cost` via diag #8811): it clamps every bar ≥ 10m, so the declared `bar_seconds × fraction` contract governs only 1m/5m frames in practice. With the tick at 83.9s mean / 187.2s max, consecutive ticks are ~144s apart, so a 60s TTL **cannot survive a tick gap for any timeframe** — the cache is a within-tick device only. Live counters: 548 hits / 1954 misses on-loop, misses **73.2% by count and 84.7% by time at ≥ 15m** — precisely the frames the cap excluded, and the largest single line is `fetch.1d` (160 on-loop + 602 off-loop fetches of DAILY bars in 2h, at 2.4–3.6s each). **The VALUE is Tier-3, separable from the knob (Tier-1) — this is why the default did not move.** Strategies read `candles_df["close"].iloc[-1]` as the CURRENT PRICE for entry geometry (`_base.py`, `trend_donchian.py`, `turtle_soup.py`, `vwap.py`, `ict_scalp.py`, …) and the monitor reads the same field for exit decisions, so the TTL bounds how stale the price behind a live order may be — it is not a chart-freshness setting. Context for choosing it: the tick's own duration already imposes 84s mean / 187s max of price staleness independent of any cache, so 60s is *stricter than the staleness the system imposes on itself*; a ~300s cap would convert the 1h/2h/4h/1d misses (18.8s per tick on-loop) into hits at a worst case of the same order the slow tick already produces, and shortening the tick shortens that inherent term too. A `*_FRACTION` cadence knob, NOT a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive), and an unparseable value falls back to the default rather than disabling — a typo must not silently switch caching off OR on. **Two caches, different risk:** (a) the CONNECTOR memo is unconditional and has no TTL — it changes no data semantics, it only stops ccxt re-downloading the full market catalogue on a fresh client (the ~3.2s/builder that made the tick 251s); **IB WAS excluded until 2026-08-22, and ⚠️ THE REASON THIS ROW GAVE FOR THE EXCLUSION WAS FACTUALLY WRONG** (corrected 2026-08-21, fixed in code 2026-08-22 by #10114, `BL-20260821-EXIT-EVAL-BREACHES-60S-ON-A-THIRD-OF-CYCLES`). It read: *"an `IBMarketData` holds a live socket on a specific clientId — sharing one is the BL-20260706-IBACCTUPDATES-COLLISION multi-client hazard."* **`IBMarketData` holds no socket.** Its `__init__` (`src/exchange/ib_connector.py:248`) sets `use_rth` + `market_data_type` and takes `self._client = get_ib_client(...)`, which is **already a process-wide registry keyed on `(host, port, client_id)`** (`src/units/accounts/ib_client.py:3501`) — so every `IBMarketData` for one endpoint ALREADY shares one `IBClient` today, and the exclusion prevents no sharing that is not already happening one layer down. Field beats comment; the code is authoritative and the prose was not. ⚠️ **AND THE ROW NEVER STATED WHAT THE EXCLUSION COSTS, which is the dangerous half:** because a fresh wrapper is built per request and `_candle_cache_key` keys on a per-OBJECT lifetime token, **every IB candle request is a guaranteed cache MISS at any TTL** — measured, one open IB 15m package fetched from the venue **281 times in 281 consecutive passes** (1.000/pass, zero hits, against a 90s TTL and a 42.19s revisit interval predicting ~132), while the three non-IB frames landed within 12% of prediction. Each miss also inserts a permanently-unhittable entry, feeding the >512 wholesale flush noted above. **So do NOT read the 1d cap analysis in this row as covering the IB frames** — raising `CANDLE_CACHE_TTL_MAX_S` to **86400** was measured and an IB frame still went to the venue **5/5** times. ✅ **THE EXCLUSION IS NOW REMOVED AND THE FIX IS LIVE-VERIFIED** — #10114 (merged 1b05353, 2026-08-22), evidence `docs/research/exit-eval-fetch-attribution-2026-08-21.md`. ⚠️ **This row previously ended "the exclusion itself is UNCHANGED in code and its removal is a Tier-3 proposal, not applied" — do not re-quote that**; it was true when written and was left standing for a day after the code moved. IB is memoized on its **RESOLVED connection identity** (`_ib_connection_identity`: host/port/clientId/account, resolved settings → env → `accounts.yaml`), not on the settings dict, so the memo key cannot drift from what would actually be constructed; an unresolvable endpoint (no `ib_port` anywhere) still refuses to memo. **It adds NO new socket sharing** — the live-IB-client count per process is unchanged for the reason above, so BL-20260706-IBACCTUPDATES-COLLISION is untouched. **Measured on the live trader** (`/api/diag/tick_cost`; **after** = one process `23:08:50Z→04:48:04Z`, n=676 passes; **before** = n=433): off-loop `fetch.15m` **1.002 → 0.494 per pass**, mean exit pass **42.3s → 4.89s (8.6×)**, `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` breaches **28.9% → 5/675 = 0.74%** of passes. ⚠️ **State the population when quoting the breach ratio:** these counters are **per-process** and the 28.9% pre-figure's own process was not recorded beside it, so the two rates are not guaranteed to share a denominator — the direction and order of magnitude are solid, the precise ~39× is approximate. ⚠️ **The 60s requirement is still BREACHED at the tail** (`max_interval_ms` 88.76s) — the residual is `BL-20260816-IB-QUEUE-TIMEOUT-EXCEEDS-EXIT-BUDGET`, a different cause; do not read the 8.6× as closing it. (b) the CANDLE cache is TTL-bounded per above, keyed on `(client instance, symbol, timeframe, limit)` so two venues serving the same symbol string never share, **never caches a `since=` request** (a historical-range reconstruction goes to the venue every time), skips unknown timeframes, and hands every caller a `.copy()` so a builder adding an indicator column cannot corrupt the next builder's frame. |
| `EXPOSURE_SOAK_SECONDS` | Sampling cadence (seconds, default `900` = 15 min; `<= 0` pauses) of the **gross-exposure observation soak** (`src/runtime/exposure_soak.py::emit_exposure_soak`, called once per trader tick from `src/main.py`). Writes one `runtime_logs/exposure_soak.jsonl` row per declared account per window — the account's own `RiskManager.report()["exposure"]`, never a reconstruction (a second definition of "exposure" would be free to drift from the one that governs). **A cadence knob, NOT an enable gate** — the `ACCOUNT_REACHABILITY_CHECK_SECONDS` / `PROP_MONITOR_PULSE_SECONDS` shape; a required observability capability must not sit behind a default-off `*_ENABLED` flag (Prime Directive), and an unparseable value **falls back to the default rather than pausing**, so a typo cannot silently switch observation off. Observe-only, connection-free, best-effort (never raises into the tick). Surfaced at `/api/bot/exposure/soak` + `/api/diag/log_file?name=exposure_soak`. |
| `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` / `EXIT_LOOP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` / `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` / `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | **The M20 exit-loop decouple** (`src/main.py::_exit_loop` + `src/runtime/exit_loop_health.py`, Tier-2 operator-approved, shipped + live-verified 2026-08-12 — `docs/sprint-logs/S-M20-EXIT-LOOP-DECOUPLE-2026-08-12.md`). Exit evaluation runs on its own **daemon loop** instead of inline on the trader tick, so a live trade is re-evaluated on the exit loop's cadence rather than once per tick. ⚠️ **DO NOT QUOTE THE GO-LIVE MARGIN — IT NO LONGER HOLDS** (corrected 2026-08-16, `BL-20260816-EXIT-EVAL-INTERVAL-AT-60S-REQUIREMENT`). This row previously read "re-evaluated every **30.2 s observed** … worst observed interval **34.1 s**, clearing the operator's 60 s ask by 43.1%". That was true at go-live and is **stale in the dangerous direction**: measured 2026-08-16T05:39Z over **n=694** passes on one process, `monitor.strategy_monitor_loop` is **28852.0 ms mean / 58940.8 ms max**. Because the interval is `max(interval, pass)` (see the period-targeting note below), the **worst observed inter-evaluation interval is 58.9 s against a 60 s requirement — 1.1 s of margin, not 43%.** ⚠️ **DO NOT REPEAT THE "MOVED ONE WAY" READING EITHER** (corrected 2026-08-16, same day, on a second process). This row briefly said "the series has moved one way throughout: 22.4 s → 34.1 s → 26.6 s → 58.9 s". **That series is not valid evidence of a trend, for two reasons.** (1) It **mixes means with maxima** as if they were one quantity: separated, the *means* are 22.4 s (n=55) → 30.2 s (go-live) → 26.6 s (n=219) → 28.9 s (n=694) → **21.6 s (n=32)**, a range of 21.6–30.2 s that goes up, down, up, down — flat, with the newest process the LOWEST of the five. The *maxima* are three points: 34.1 s, 58.9 s, 41.5 s. (2) It **compares maxima across different n**, and a maximum grows with sample size even from a fixed distribution — so `max(n=694) > max(n=38)` is the expected result of drawing more samples, not evidence of degradation. **What survives is that 58940.8 ms was really observed**: a live trade did go 58.9 s unevaluated. The supported diagnosis is a **fat tail finally sampled**, not a system creeping toward the requirement — which points at bounding the tail (`BL-20260816-IB-QUEUE-TIMEOUT-EXCEEDS-EXIT-BUDGET`) rather than hunting for what got slower. ✅ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-25 AND THE TAIL WAS BOUND — the 58.9 s / 1.1 s-margin figures above are HISTORY, do not quote them as current.** Population, because it is the whole point: **n = 991 intervals across 10 processes over 8.3 h** (09:37Z→17:56Z), read from `/api/diag/log_file?name=exit_interval_soak` — the DURABLE per-pass soak, not a single process's in-memory max, so this is the first reading not subject to the "no process lived long enough to draw the tail" caveat two sentences above. `interval_ms` **29879 mean / 29970 median / 33476 p95 / 45034 MAX**, and `over_requirement` **0 of 991 (0.00%)** — so the worst observed inter-evaluation interval is **45.0 s against the 60 s requirement, 15.0 s of margin**. The pass itself is **2222 ms mean / 19481 ms max**, against 28852 ms mean / 58940.8 ms max on 2026-08-16 — a **13×** improvement in the mean, from #10114's IB connector memo plus `IB_PROBE_CACHE_S`. The interval is now bounded by the **30 s cadence**, not by the pass, which is the regime the design intends. ⚠️ **TWO THINGS THIS DOES NOT SAY.** (1) It is a 1000-row TAIL of a 6.29 MB file, so it is the last 8.3 h and not the lifetime; the max over the whole file may be higher. (2) A 45.0 s interval is NOT explained by the 19.5 s max pass — `max(cadence, pass)` would cap at 30 s — so ~15 s came from a stall BETWEEN the sleep and the next completion, exactly the gap the measured completion-to-completion interval exists to catch and the derivation would miss. Small against the requirement today; it is the term to watch if the margin shrinks again. **The MAX is the quantity the requirement is written against, not the mean** — a mean-only reading is what let this drift unremarked, and it is why `BL-20260814-EXIT-PASS-SLOWER-THAN-M20` was closed while the max kept growing. **The requirement is now GRADED, and this is the second, separate threshold** (shipped 2026-08-16 — before that, nothing alarmed on it: `exit_loop_health` graded only `fresh`/`stale` on `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS`, so a 59 s pass read healthy and the requirement was observable only by reading `max_ms` out of `/api/diag/tick_cost` by hand). `EXIT_EVAL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default `60`) is the **requirement**; `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` (180 s) is **liveness**. They are deliberately different questions and must never be collapsed into one number — at 180 s a 59 s interval and a 179 s interval are both `fresh`. `exit_loop_health` now MEASURES the completion-to-completion interval (**not** the `max(interval, pass)` derivation — a stall between the sleep and the next completion is invisible to the derivation) and publishes `max_interval_ms` + a **`requirement_state`** registered with `collapsed-state-guard`. ⚠️ **IT IS FIVE STATES AS OF 2026-09-09 AND THIS ROW SAID FOUR UNTIL THEN — do not re-quote the four.** ∈ `within` / **`near_miss`** / `breached` / `not_measured` (fewer than 2 passes, so no interval EXISTS — never `within`) / `unknown`. **`near_miss`** (`max_interval_ms / requirement_ms >= 0.9`) was added on an operator decision (`WO-20260909-DECISION-M20-EXIT-EVAL-MARGIN-COLLAPSED`, `chosen: near_miss_and_restart_gap`; audit F-50) because the grade was **binary about the thing that matters**: MEASURED 2026-09-09 over **n=989 intervals across 12 processes** (08:36:14Z–16:54:41Z; mean 29880.6, median 29993.1, p95 34886.9, MAX **59951.2**, `over_requirement` 0 of 1000), the worst gap cleared the 60 s promise by **48.8 ms** and every instrument read `within` — identically to a comfortable 20 s max. ⚠️ **That is NOT a margin that has been shrinking and must not be quoted as one**: against 2026-08-25 (n=991, same window shape) the distribution BODY is unmoved (mean 29879 → 29880.6, median 29970 → 29993.1) and only the tail moved (MAX 45034 → 59951.2). ⚠️ **And the requirement was already BREACHED once, on 2026-09-07T22:48:54Z** — recorded fact from `requirement_breach_process` in the alert latch, which is written ONLY when a breach fires the alert. `near_miss` is a 🟡 **WARN**, latched per process on **its own key** beside the breach latch (so a process that warns and later breaches produces BOTH), and is deliberately **NOT a widening of `breached`** — no trade went unevaluated past the requirement, and spending the ALERT vocabulary on a promise that was KEPT is how the operator gets trained past the one that means it was not. The producer tests `breached` FIRST, so the band can never downgrade a real breach. `requirement_ratio` ships beside it and is **`None`, never `0.0`**, when no interval exists. 0.9 is the **operator's chosen number, not a tuned one**, and is deliberately **not an env knob** — it is the recorded content of a decision, and a knob would make it silently disableable. **`state: fresh` and `requirement_state: breached` can coexist**, which is exactly the condition that was previously unrepresentable. A breach alerts **once per PROCESS** — `max_interval_ms` resets on restart, so a global latch would go silent after the first breach ever. ⚠️ **Read `requirement_state` beside `intervals_measured`, never alone** (the `max_multiple`/`measured_n` discipline): the grade is per-process and the trader restarts on every merge to `main` — three processes in ~8.5 h on 2026-08-16 — while the tail needs a large `n` to be drawn at all (the 58.9 s observation came from an n=694 overnight process; the two daytime processes reached n=38 and n=23, maxing at 41.5 s and 47.4 s). On a busy day `within` can mean *"no process lived long enough to draw the tail"*, **not** *"the requirement was met today"*. The pass is **fetch-bound** (off-loop `fetchby.strategy_monitor_loop` n=10034, the largest fetch consumer anywhere; off-loop `fetch.1d` mean 10720.9 ms), so this shares a root with the tick regression and a TTL/fetch change aimed at the tick reaches **exit decisions** too, in both directions. **Only the EXIT half moved** — the 13 reconcilers stay on the tick, because only the exit half is what the 60 s ask is about. `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` truthy is the **sanctioned rollback: one env flip + restart, no redeploy** — no thread, the tick calls `run_monitor_tick`, byte-for-byte the old behaviour. `EXIT_LOOP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default `30`) is **period-targeting, not sleep-after** (`slack = interval − elapsed`), so the inter-evaluation interval is `max(interval, pass)` and a pass that overruns starts the next immediately rather than queueing — the cadence is a floor on frequency, never a schedule to catch up on (piling passes onto one shared IB socket is how the June 2026 wedges started). `EXIT_LOOP_STALE_SECONDS` (default `180`) is when `exit_loop_health` grades the loop `stale`. **BOTH DEFAULTS ARE CHOSEN, NOT MEASURED** — bounded by the 22.4 s mean pass with one 55-pass distribution behind them; do not read them as tuned values. The loop's liveness is **only** observable via `exit_loop_health` (`/api/diag/log_file?name=exit_loop_health`), because the decouple took exit evaluation OUT of the liveness watchdog's coverage — that coverage was never a probe, it was the fact that exit evaluation ran INLINE on the tick whose heartbeat the watchdog measures. Its four **liveness** states are never collapsed: `unknown` / `never_ran` (**emphatically NOT healthy** — also what a set `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` produces) / `fresh` / `stale`; read them beside the **five** requirement states described above, which answer a different question. ⚠️ **AND NEITHER SET COVERS THE RESTART GAP — the one interval the 60 s promise covers and every per-process instrument excludes BY CONSTRUCTION.** `max_interval_ms` is a module global that resets on restart, and the trader restarts on every merge to `main` (three processes in ~8.5 h on 2026-08-16; **twelve in ~8.3 h on 2026-09-09**), so the gap from the last completed pass of process N to the first of process N+1 was measured by **nobody** — and a deploy is exactly when the trader is least likely to be evaluating exits (old process stopped, new one booting with every cache cold, the `BL-20260609-001` shape). `src/runtime/exit_restart_gap.py` (2026-09-09, same operator decision) grades it against the same requirement, with its own five-state **`restart_gap_state`** ∈ `within` / `near_miss` / `breached` / `not_measured` / `unknown`, registered with `collapsed-state-guard`. It can only live in the **durable soak** — a cross-process quantity cannot be computed by a process — so the read surface is `summary.restart_gap` on **`/api/bot/exit-interval/soak`** (computed over every row on disk), while the live trader grades **only its own boundary, once per process**, off a bounded tail read (a restart gap joins two ADJACENT rows, so reading 6 MB on a tick would be cost for nothing). ⚠️ **`not_measured` here means fewer than two processes are in the population, so no boundary EXISTS — it is NOT compliance**; `unknown` means boundaries existed and none could be graded, typically a ROTATED log whose successor's earliest surviving row is not a genuine first pass, where grading anyway would yield an arbitrary WITHIN-process interval wearing a restart-gap label — systematically SHORT, i.e. wrong in the reassuring direction. An `overlapping` boundary (the old process completed a pass during handover) is **named and excluded from the max, never clamped to 0 ms**. ⚠️ **A gap is not proof of a restart**: if `EXIT_LOOP_DECOUPLE_DISABLED` was set for a window nothing wrote to the soak at all, and the boundary either side renders as one enormous "restart gap" — read `from_utc`/`to_utc` rather than inventing a plausibility threshold that silently reclassifies large gaps. **MEASURED 2026-09-09 the gaps were clean**: 11 of them at **25.3–53.9 s, 0 of 11 over 60 s, 1.3% of wall clock** — the finding was never that they were long, it was that nothing was watching them. |
| `TRADE_OPEN_BANNER_WINDOW_MIN` | Window (minutes, default `30`) for the best-effort **trade_open** info banner on `GET /api/bot/notifications` — a compact "N trades opened in the last Nm" line so a fresh entry surfaces on the app Overview banner. Read-only DB query, best-effort (any failure omits the banner). |
| `WORK_DECISION_PROMPT_SECONDS` / `WORK_DECISION_API_BASE` / `WORK_DECISION_PROMPT_RETAIN_DAYS` | Knobs for the **two-way Telegram decision channel** (`src/runtime/telegram_decisions.py`, operator-requested 2026-09-02: *"if there's decisions that are pop-ups they can be given to me as pop-ups in the telegram chat ... instead of having to see the ping and then open the session"*). A sweep on `ict-telegram-bot.service`'s job queue reads `GET /api/bot/work/decisions` and sends ONE inline-keyboard prompt per un-prompted, `not_submitted` request; a tap POSTs to `/api/bot/work/decision` over loopback with the `DASHBOARD_API_TOKEN` bearer. ⚠️ **IT DECIDES NOTHING AND THE CONFIRMATION SAYS SO** — the route returns `answerState: in_transit`, so the reply after a tap reads *"Submitted — NOT yet decided"* and names the object YAML where truth lands. A confirmation that said *answered* would be the forward failure the transit contract exists to refuse. ⚠️ **THE ROUTE STAYS THE ONE OWNER OF EVERY REFUSAL** — unknown option (400), empty submission (400), already-answered (**409**, rendered to the operator as *already answered*, not as an error), fail-closed write gate (503). The bot re-implements none of them; it translates the answer for a human. ⚠️ **`callback_data` is 26 bytes FIXED — `wdec:<12 hex>:<8 hex>`** against Telegram's 64-byte cap, digesting `(object_id, request_id)` and the option **KEY**. The option token is a key digest and **never a positional index**: an index is stable only while nobody edits the object file, so a reordering between the send and the tap would silently record a DIFFERENT answer than the one whose label the operator read; a key digest fails loudly (`option_gone`). A digest collision is **refused**, never resolved by first match. ⚠️ **MULTIPLE CHOICE ONLY — free text is a second step and is deliberately not half-built.** Inline buttons cannot carry arbitrary text; a request declaring no options is still SENT (so a question the operator is blocking on is not invisible) but says plainly that it needs an answer on the SPA or in the repo, and the two populations are counted separately in the sweep stats. ⚠️ **DESTINATION IS THE *POLLED* BOT, NOT `claude_route()`.** Delivery and ANSWERABILITY are different properties — a `sendMessage` needs only a token, while an inline-keyboard BUTTON is inert unless a process POLLS that bot AND has a `CallbackQueryHandler` for the prefix (the tap emits a `callback_query` nobody collects, with no error on any surface). `telegram_routes` reasons only about the first, which is why `telegram_decisions.answerable_route()` exists beside it rather than reusing it. ⚠️ **THIS ROW READ "`TELEGRAM_CLAUDE_BOT_SECRET` … is polled by **nothing**" UNTIL 2026-09-02 AND THAT IS NOW FALSE — do not re-quote it.** It was a correct measurement of the shipped state and became stale the moment the polling half landed: `src/bot/claude_decision_bot.py` + `deploy/ict-claude-decision-bot.service` now poll the dedicated bot and register the SAME `wdec` handler (imported from `telegram_decisions`, never forked — the route stays the one owner of every refusal). `answerable_route()` prefers the Claude bot **only on positive poll evidence** from `src/runtime/telegram_poll_registry.py`, whose three states are never collapsed: `polled_with_handler` (a live process claims it polls this token AND handles this prefix) · `token_only_not_polled` (we LOOKED; a tap would not be received) · `unknown` (we could NOT look — fails closed, but stays a distinct value). Evidence is a **heartbeat a live poller refreshes**, so a claim expires with the process that made it; an env var asserting "this is polled" would be a guard cheaper to lie to than to satisfy. Registered with `collapsed-state-guard` as `telegram_poll.poll_state`. ⚠️ **IT DEGRADES RATHER THAN GOING SILENT:** with the Claude bot unconfirmed, prompts fall back to the TRADER bot — the wrong chat, LOUDLY and countably (`destination` + `poll_state` in the sweep stats, a WARNING naming the state-specific REMEDY, and a startup banner per bot naming which token it polls and which prefixes it handles). A wrong chat is a noise complaint; holding every decision prompt is an outage. Only when NEITHER bot is confirmed polled does the sweep hold, counted as `held_not_polled` and never pooled with `held_route` (no destination at all) — different faults, different fixes. ⚠️ **`ict-claude-decision-bot.service` is NOT `ict-claude-bridge.service`**, whose name is historical: that one polls the **PROP** token. And `TELEGRAM_CLAUDE_BOT_TOKEN` drives the PROP bot despite its name — the dedicated bot is `TELEGRAM_CLAUDE_BOT_SECRET`. ⚠️ **UNPROVEN ON THE FLEET:** no tap has been observed on ClaudeBot; a green harness cannot reach that, and the service must be installed + enabled and the secret present in the VM `.env` first. `..._SECONDS` (default `300`) is the cadence and `<= 0` PAUSES prompting; an unparseable value falls back to the **default, never to zero** — a typo must not silently switch the decision channel off. `..._API_BASE` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8001`) is where the sweep reads and writes; `..._RETAIN_DAYS` (default `30`) bounds the prompted-marker file. Cadence/scope knobs, **not** a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive). Marker state at `/api/diag/log_file?name=work_decision_prompted`. ⚠️ **THE MARKER RECORDS *WHERE THE PROMPT WENT* AS OF 2026-09-04 (MI-109), AND UNTIL THEN IT DID NOT.** Its entry was `{prompted_at, object_id, request_id, kind}` and named no destination, while the fallback above may legitimately send to the TRADER bot — so a prompt delivered to the wrong chat was **permanently consumed** by a once-only marker and unattributable afterwards. Operator-raised twice in two hours on 2026-09-03: *"I'm still not seeing decisions in the bot"* — with the channel UP, the write gate OPEN, and the marker asserting a confirmed send. Each row now carries `destination` + `poll_state` + `token_from` + `chat_from` — the token **VARIABLE** name, **never a token value**, since the file is on a read surface. ⚠️ **AND A PROMPT THAT MISSED THE PREFERRED BOT IS RE-SENT EXACTLY ONCE, NEVER UNCONDITIONALLY.** All three must hold: the marker does NOT record `claude`, the route IS now `claude`, and this request has never been re-sent — so the retry is spent only on a strictly better destination and can never loop. The three suppressing verdicts (`suppressed_reached_preferred` / `suppressed_route_not_better` / `suppressed_already_redelivered`) are counted separately in the sweep stats. A marker predating the field grades `unrecorded` — ***we did not look***, deliberately neither of the other two: folding it into `reached_preferred` would leave every already-consumed prompt unrecoverable, and into `wrong_destination` would assert a mis-delivery nobody observed. A duplicate DECISION stays impossible regardless (the route's 409). ⚠️ **THE PER-RUN STATS ARE NOW DURABLE — `/api/diag/log_file?name=work_decision_sweep_receipt`.** `destination` and `poll_state` WERE logged, to the systemd journal only, whose retention was **measured at ~30 minutes** (a 1500-line pull spanned 14:23:34→14:53:46), so the 14:05:48 send was unreachable 45 minutes later — the evidence needed to diagnose a mis-delivery was gone before anyone was told there was one. More journal logging was therefore explicitly NOT the fix. ⚠️ **It is a BOUNDED RING (200 rows), not a one-slot receipt like `work_digest_receipt.json`** — that carrier is HOURLY while this sweep fires every 300s, so one slot would retain FIVE MINUTES, worse than the journal it replaces; 200 rows is ~16.7h. ⚠️ **Stamped on EVERY outcome** — `paused`, an unreadable inbox, an unreadable prompt-state and a raised exception all land there, because a receipt written only on a send cannot tell a DEAD sweep from a FAILING one. An ABSENT file means the sweep has never run on this VM; it never means nothing is waiting. ⚠️ **LANDED, NOT PROVEN:** arming it needs an `ict-telegram-bot.service` restart (Tier-2), and no prompt has yet been OBSERVED carrying its destination. ⚠️ **THE SWEEP IS NO LONGER THE ONLY WAY IN — `/decisions` PULLS THE SAME INBOX ON DEMAND** (2026-09-02, operator-requested: *"if there's any decisions that are still waiting for me that aren't answered so they can all pop up at once. In case I don't get them as they come in"*). It reuses the SAME `render_decision_prompt` / `build_decision_keyboard` builders, so there is still exactly one author of `callback_data`. ⚠️ **IT NEITHER READS NOR WRITES THE PROMPTED MARKER, DELIBERATELY** — not reading is the point of the ask (it must re-send what the sweep already asked), and not writing keeps the sweep's once-only behaviour intact: writing a marker here would SUPPRESS the sweep for a question the operator pulled, so a failed on-demand send would leave it asked by nobody. The accepted consequence is stated rather than hidden — a pulled question may ALSO arrive once from the sweep — because a duplicate PROMPT is recoverable and a suppressed one is not, and the route's 409 makes a duplicate DECISION impossible. **Tier-2** (it touches the Telegram bridge and needs an `ict-telegram-bot.service` restart); no order path, no strategy config, no risk caps. |
| `CLOSE_WEDGE_INEFFECTIVE_CANCEL_DISABLED` | **The one-flip rollback for the SECOND evidenced-unclearable determination** (`src/runtime/close_wedge_standing.py::is_unclearable`, Tier-2, operator-approved 2026-09-08). Alpaca returns a SUCCESS code for a cancel it does not perform: the live `alpaca_paper`/GLD give-up at 2026-09-08T14:12:54Z read `cancel: accepted=2 already_gone=0 refused=0`, and ~6.0s later BOTH orders still rested with `canceled_at: None`, status `new` and `held` — not even moved to `pending_cancel`. `classify_share_hold` reads STATUSES, so it graded that `orders_still_resting` — *"a retry may clear it"* — **181 times over seven days about orders unmoved since 2026-08-27**. The new state `cancel_accepted_ineffective` is that contradiction between two broker endpoints INSIDE ONE ATTEMPT: an id we asked to cancel, that the broker ACCEPTED (2xx), still in the residual listing with `canceled_at` null and a non-wedged status. ⚠️ **IT IS A SEPARATE STATE ON MEASURED GROUNDS, NOT A WIDENING OF `broker_cancel_wedged`** — over 2026-09-04 → 2026-09-08 (n=122 GLD `close_failure` rows) **63 PAGES carried `orders_still_resting`** against 57 digest + 2 pages on `broker_cancel_wedged`, so the first determination provably cannot reach them; that measurement is why `UNCLEARABLE_HOLD_STATE` became the SET `UNCLEARABLE_HOLD_STATES` rather than the existing test being loosened. ⚠️ **IT KEYS ON THE EVIDENCED DETERMINATION ONLY, NEVER ON THE FAILURE REPEATING** — a close that has failed a thousand times for a reason nobody established is still retried every tick, and a test pins that. ⚠️ **IT IS A CADENCE, NOT A STOP**: the wedge is re-probed on `CLOSE_WEDGE_REPROBE_MINUTES`, which is what keeps the ledger's `last_seen` alive so `sweep_vanished` cannot manufacture a `vanished_unattributed` for a position that never moved, and what re-arms the retry the instant the venue changes. ⚠️ **THE FIRST SIGHTING STILL PAGES** (`newly_wedged` is a LOUD transition — a position becoming unclearable is news exactly once); only `still_standing` is quiet, and even that is floored at 24h. **Truthy here is the ROLLBACK: routing and retry return byte-for-byte to what they were before this state existed, no redeploy.** ⚠️ **It disables the BINDING and NOT the MEASUREMENT** — the classifier still emits `cancel_accepted_ineffective`, and the give-up log, the retMsg and the `operator_alerts` ring still carry it, so the rows a reviewer needs keep accruing while the state decides nothing. That asymmetry is deliberate and is the same one the account allowlists use (`NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS`'s 2026-08-09 correction): scoping the binding without scoping the measurement is what makes a held-back state reviewable instead of invisible. Only an explicit `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` rolls it back — a typo must not silently disarm what the operator approved. Read at call time. |
| `TELEGRAM_POLL_STALE_SECONDS` / `TELEGRAM_POLL_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS` | How long a poll claim stays trustworthy (default `900`) and how often a polling process refreshes it (default `120`) — `src/runtime/telegram_poll_registry.py`. The claim is a **heartbeat, not a declaration**: a process that dies, hangs or is stopped stops refreshing and its assertion expires on its own, which is what makes "is this bot polled?" evidence rather than an assertion. An env var saying `TELEGRAM_CLAUDE_BOT_POLLED=1` would have been one line and would have been a lie the moment the unit was masked. ⚠️ **The window is generous on purpose** — the refresh job shares an event loop with the poller, so a slow update is normal, and a too-tight window would report a healthy bot as dead and push every operator decision onto the wrong channel each time it fired (the desensitised-alarm shape, with a routing consequence). ⚠️ **An unparseable OR non-positive value falls back to the DEFAULT, never to zero**: a zero window would expire every heartbeat instantly and permanently reroute the decision channel, so a typo must not be able to reach that state. Tuning knobs, **not** a default-off `*_ENABLED` gate (Prime Directive). Claims are readable at `/api/diag/log_file?name=telegram_poll_claude` and `…=telegram_poll_trader`; each names the token **VARIABLE** and never a value. |

Once journal and venue diverge the divergence is **PERMANENT BY CONSTRUCTION** (`BL-20260823-MODIFY-IDEMPOTENCE-COMPARES-INTENT-TO-JOURNAL-NEVER-TO-VENUE`). Motivating instance, **now RESOLVED — do not re-quote it as live** (verified 2026-08-29): `ib_paper` MES 4350 declared 7533.69642857 while its only resting stop sat at 7516.50 — 69 ticks, **$1,289.73** on 15 contracts — held from 2026-08-20 with a healthy monitor on a connected session. What closed the gap is NOT established; the candidates are an ordinary re-arm on a subsequent open or a naked-sweep repair, and neither was observed. So the row's STRUCTURAL claim is untouched and still the point — an idempotence filter comparing intent to the JOURNAL rather than the VENUE makes a divergence permanent by construction — while its one live exhibit is gone. ⚠️ **THIS ROW READ "The gate is therefore still UNEXERCISED: no soak row has yet recorded a `reassert` verdict on a real position" UNTIL 2026-09-08, AND BOTH HALVES ARE NOW FALSE — do not re-quote it.** Measured 2026-09-08 over the WHOLE `protection_reassert_soak` file (17 rows) via `/api/diag/log_file?name=protection_reassert_soak`: **15 rows are `mode: annotate` / `apply_scope: not_allowlisted` / `acted: false`, and TWO are `mode: apply` / `apply_scope: allowlisted` / `acted: true`** — `ib_paper` MES (2026-08-23T13:54:32Z) and `ib_paper` MGC trade 5531 (2026-09-07T11:55:19Z, `applied_state: both_legs_resting`, `ocaGroup oca-protect-t5531`). So the gate is ARMED on an allowlisted account and has cancelled-and-re-placed a live bracket twice; it is EXERCISED, not merely deployed. ⚠️ **AND THE SECOND EXERCISE IS NOT A CLEAN SUCCESS STORY** — it re-armed a 43-lot bracket for a journal row the broker does not back (journal MGC 54 vs venue 11), which is the mechanism working correctly on bad input rather than a fault in the gate; see `BL-20260908-IB-PAPER-MGC-JOURNAL-HOLDS-43-LOTS-THE-BROKER-DOES-NOT-AND-A-43-LOT-STOP-RESTS-AGAINST-THE-PHANTOM`. ⚠️ The `ships at annotate / allowlist empty` sentence earlier in this row describes the state as it stood on 2026-08-29, when it was the reason the MES exhibit could not be credited to this gate; it is NOT a statement of the current live value. Read the live arm with `get-env` against `/proc/<MainPID>/environ`, never from this row. **It rides the cadence-gated naked sweep** (`IB_BROKER_NAKED_CHECK_SECONDS`, 300s), which ALREADY fetches the resting prices, so it adds **no broker read** — the obvious alternative (compare against the venue inside the per-pass modify path) would put a broker call on every open position every pass, which is the shape of both June 2026 wedges. **The DECISION is a pure function** so the policy is arguable in tests rather than against a live position — which is exactly what went wrong on 2026-08-20 (`BL-20260820-OVERCOVER-REMEDIATION-CANCELLED-THE-JOURNAL-MATCHING-LEG`). Seven states, never collapsed: `reassert` · `agrees` · `not_graded` (**we did not look** — an unresolvable tick or unreadable resting price is NOT agreement) · `suppressed_cooldown` · `suppressed_attempts` · `needs_both_legs` · `position_absent`. ⚠️ **THE LEVEL IS ALWAYS THE JOURNAL'S** (`trades.stop_loss` / `take_profit_1`), never a caller's or an operator's — recording the venue's stray level as the new declaration would launder it into the journal and make the divergence unfindable. ⚠️ **BOTH LEGS OR NEITHER**: `modify_protective` re-arms the WHOLE bracket, so a re-assert carrying only the stop would drop a resting target; a missing declared level is a REFUSAL (`needs_both_legs`), not a half-arm. ⚠️ **AN EMPTY `..._ACCOUNTS` ALLOWLIST MEANS *NONE*, DELIBERATELY THE OPPOSITE OF ITS SIBLINGS** — `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS` and `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS` both read empty as ALL, which this file already flags as *"not a safe default, it is the widest one"*; those widen a size and a DB write, this one cancels and re-places a live position's exit, so an unset variable must not arm an order path on every account including real money. | `PROTECTION_STRAY_GROUP_MODE` | **Clear a trade's OWN prior protection resting under a DIFFERENT group name** (`off` / `annotate` (default) / `apply`; `src/runtime/stray_oca_groups.py` + `ib_client._sweep_stray_oca_groups`, 2026-08-26). `place_protective`'s keyed pre-cancel is scoped **BY NAME** to `oca-protect-t<oca_key>` (`_cancel_oca_group_for_symbol` skips any leg whose `ocaGroup` differs), so when a trade's earlier bracket rests under a legacy `oca-protect-<reqId>` name — or the **bare-numeric** form `834864174` measured live on MGC — that group is never a cancellation candidate and the re-arm mints a SECOND, non-mutually-cancelling group beside it. ⚠️ **THIS IS NOT `BL-20260825-PLACE-PROTECTIVE-COUNTS-THE-CANCEL-CALL-NOT-ITS-EFFECT` AND MUST NOT BE FOLDED INTO IT** — that row is the *symbol-wide fallback* (no `oca_key` → Error 10147 → mint), and all three of its defects are already fixed in `b81458a4`, which is an ancestor of the deployed sha. Captured in the act 2026-08-26T02:08:35Z on `ib_paper`/MHG: a routine trailing amend (`verdict={'sl': 6.31757143}`) armed `oca-protect-t4796` beside `oca-protect-446` + `oca-protect-465` → **300%** at 02:09:36Z, with **no** `no oca_key` warning, **no** 10147 and **no** survivor-join log. ⚠️ **It mints ONCE PER TRADE, at the legacy→keyed transition, so it is FINITE** — every trade opened since the keyed path shipped is keyed from the start; an earlier reading of this as an unbounded generator was wrong. **The rule is *cancel NON-KEYED groups*, never *cancel every other group***: IB nets per contract, so one symbol legitimately hosts one protective group per open trade, and a symbol-wide cancel from one trade's amend would strip a sibling's take-profit (`BL-20260814-IB-PROTECTION-BOOLEAN-NOT-QUANTITY`). A **keyed** group carries its owning trade id in its own name, so a sibling's group is self-identifying and preserved by construction — with **no journal read on the order path**. Five states, never collapsed: `keep_target` · `sibling_keyed` (PRESERVE) · `stray_unkeyed` (the finding) · **`ungrouped` — REPORTED, NEVER CANCELLED** (a leg with no `ocaGroup` cannot be shown to be this trade's abandoned protection and could be a hand-placed exit; the same refusal `attach-ib-target` makes) · `not_protective`. The DECISION is a **pure function** so the policy is arguable in tests rather than against a live position — the lesson of `BL-20260820-OVERCOVER-REMEDIATION-CANCELLED-THE-JOURNAL-MATCHING-LEG`. Runs **only on the keyed path** (an absent `oca_key` is the fallback path and is deliberately not widened from here) and reads **`ib.openTrades()` directly, NOT `self._open_trades`**, because that helper swallows the exception and returns `[]`, collapsing *we could not look* into *nothing rests* (`BL-20260826-OPEN-TRADES-COLLAPSES-A-READ-FAILURE-INTO-AN-EMPTY-BOOK`). So the sweep is ARMED on `ib_paper` and has cancelled real resting legs twice. The other 6 rows are `no_strays`, which is the healthy case. ⚠️ This also supersedes `BL-20260906-STRAY-UNKEYED-OCA-GROUPS-HAVE-RETURNED-ON-TWO-OF-THREE-IB-SYMBOLS-AND-THE-SWEEP-HAS-ACTED-ONCE-EVER`'s headline: the sweep has now acted TWICE, not once. **Tier-2 to flip to `apply`**, and stage it on `ib_paper` via **`PROTECTION_STRAY_GROUP_ACCOUNTS`**. ⚠️ **AN EMPTY ALLOWLIST MEANS *NONE*, deliberately the OPPOSITE of `CONVICTION_SIZING_ACCOUNTS` / `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS`** — those widen a size and a DB write and read empty as ALL, which this file already calls *"not a safe default, it is the widest one"*; this one CANCELS a live position's resting protective legs, so an unset variable must not arm it everywhere. It copies `PROTECTION_REASSERT_ACCOUNTS`'s polarity on purpose; do not "harmonise" it back. **An absent or unnamed `account_id` never cancels** — a caller that cannot name its account cannot arm an order path. ⚠️ **The allowlist scopes the CANCEL, never the MEASUREMENT**: a held-back account is still read and still annotated, so the rows a reviewer needs before widening actually exist — the correction `NETTING_ATTRIBUTION_ACCOUNTS` needed on 2026-08-09, where intersecting the account set at the top of the pass made the account being staged TOWARD invisible.
